This is a hallmark signature of **serine hydrolases** (serine proteases, esterases, lipases, etc.). The GxSxG motif (or GDS variant) is the canonical nucleophile motif where the serine hydroxyl group performs the nucleophilic attack on the substrate&#x27;s carbonyl carbon.<br><br>While the catalytic triad of serine hydrolases typically includes Ser-His-Asp, the **nucleophile** specifically refers to the residue that directly attacks the substrate — which is the **serine**. The histidine and aspartate serve as general base/acid helpers that orient and polarize the serine, but the nucleophilic attack is performed by the serine itself.<br><br>The serine at position 154, embedded in the GDS motif, is the catalytic nucleophile of this enzyme.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> A nucleophile uses a reactive side-chain atom to attack a reaction partner, whereas other catalytic residues may participate in proton transfer. Which candidate has the nucleophilic role in this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Serine at position 154<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Aspartate at position 390<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Histidine at position 393<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Aspartate at position 367<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> active_site_0000:2, active_site_0002:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Looking at the protein sequence, I need to identify the nucleophilic residue in the catalytic center.<br><br>**Key motif identification:**<br>At positions 152-156, I find the sequence &quot;GDSMA&quot; (Gly152-Asp153-Ser154-Met155-Ala156). In serine hydrolases, it is always the serine in the GxSxG motif that acts as the nucleophile. The histidine and aspartate residues participate in proton transfer (base/acid catalysis), not nucleophilic attack.<br><br>The GDS motif at positions 152-154 is a well-established hallmark of the nucleophilic serine in these enzymes.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>

<pre>Let me analyze this protein sequence to determine its cellular component localization.

First, let me look at the sequence characteristics:

1. **N-terminal region**: &quot;MNINEILKKLINKSDLEINEAEELAKAIIRGEVPEILVSAILVALRMKGE&quot;
   - The N-terminal region shows characteristics of a mitochondrial targeting sequence (MTS):
     - Rich in hydroxylated amino acids (S, T)
     - Rich in positively charged amino acids (K, R)
     - Low in acidic amino acids (D, E) - though there are some early on
     - The segment &quot;VSAILVALR&quot; has some hydrophobic character, which is consistent with a mitochondrial targeting sequence that can form an amphipathic helix

2. **Overall composition**: 
   - The protein is relatively small (~250 amino acids)
   - Contains motifs like &quot;GEPGIDEVSPIGNTFMKIVSKR&quot; and &quot;GDFREGYEYADHLIEK&quot; which suggest an enoyl-CoA hydratase domain
   - The sequence &quot;GDFREGYEY&quot; is characteristic of enoyl-CoA hydratases, which are involved in fatty acid beta-oxidation

3. **Functional analysis**:
   - This appears to be an enoyl-CoA hydratase/isomerase family protein
   - These proteins are typically involved in fatty acid beta-oxidation
   - In eukaryotes, beta-oxidation of fatty acids occurs in the mitochondrial matrix (and peroxisomes)
   - The presence of a mitochondrial targeting sequence at the N-terminus strongly suggests mitochondrial localization

4. **Evaluating each option**:
   - A. Cytosol: Some enoyl-CoA hydratases are cytosolic, but the N-terminal sequence suggests mitochondrial targeting
   - B. Mitochondrial matrix: The N-terminal sequence has characteristics of a mitochondrial targeting signal (amphipathic helix with positive charges), and the protein appears to be an enoyl-CoA hydratase involved in beta-oxidation, which occurs in the mitochondrial matrix
   - C. Periplasm: This is a bacterial compartment (between inner and outer membranes in gram-negative bacteria). The sequence doesn&#x27;t show typical bacterial signal peptides or lipoprotein signals for periplasmic localization.
   - D. Plasma membrane: The protein doesn&#x27;t show transmembrane domains characteristic of membrane proteins. It appears to be a soluble enzyme.

The N-terminal mitochondrial targeting sequence (MTS) features:
- &quot;MNINEILKKLINKSDLEINEAEELAKAIIRGEVPEILVSAILVALRMKGE&quot;
- The initial methionine followed by a mix of charged and hydrophobic residues
- The region &quot;VSAILVALR&quot; could form an amphipathic helix
- Overall the N-terminal ~30 residues look like they could be cleaved as a transit peptide

Combined with the enzymatic function (enoyl-CoA hydratase = beta-oxidation), this strongly points to the mitochondrial matrix.

The answer is B. Mitochondrial matrix.
